| D-126 | design | 2026-08-01 | **About thirty notification generators exist or are designed, with no catalogue, no classes and no item contract.** Inventoried 2026-08-01 in `NOTIFICATIONS.md` at Bob's direction: clock-driven (overdue successor, temporal expectation, bias debt, recheck sweeps, fallback eligibility, capture-session TTL), data-flow driven (undetermined authority, source modified/removed, governed-vs-unreachable, duplicates, link verdicts, post-hoc reuse divergence, completed walked-away captures, partial captures, text-undetermined, client-rendered shells), governance (endorsements, expertise confirmations, the doorbell, export notification, owner rescue, spent invitations) and analysis (assistant-surfaced focuses, missing predecessors, improvable connection grades, satisfaction gaps, measure decay). THE SIZE IS THE ARGUMENT: thirty ad-hoc sites means thirty invented wordings, severities and button sets, and the drift is guaranteed rather than likely. This is the lesson `CONSTRUCTS.md` item 5 already recorded — ad hoc event strings with no registry, 'the same lesson the check catalogue already taught the plane' — so kinds take stable `N-` ids beside the `C-` ids, and this closes that half of D-68. CLASSES ARE THREE, NOT FOUR SEVERITIES: FINDING (true about the world/record, may become evidence), OBLIGATION (a named person must act for the record to proceed), CONDITION (a fact about our own machinery). A severity ladder was rejected because it encodes how LOUD rather than what it MEANS and rots in one direction — everything becomes a warning; warning/error are severity, which is orthogonal and mostly derivable. FINDING and CONDITION must not merge, or our plumbing dilutes the record's findings and the standing rule that a technical complication is never surfaced as a choice is broken. NEEDED: the catalogue with ids, the item contract (summary, detail, BASIS, producer-published options — never a surface-side map, which is the `op=searchfields` drift), and the `per-item` application weight beside `refuse`/`report`, where a retained item carries its reason. | CLOSED 2026-09-21 AS A DEBT ROW by LED-7 (SCHEDULER #5) — PLACED as a BACKLOG task under its own id, D-126 (owner RECORD, then UI: ONE row, BOB #19's decomposition), after UI-71, as BOB #19's inbox entry asked. Verified before placing: `NOTIFICATIONS.md` §Applying a handler to a selection designs the weight, and `affordances.mjs` publishes `refuse` 5×, `report` 1× and `single` 14× and no `per-item` (2026-09-21). The 26 designed-not-built generators are NOT placed by this: each stays under its own originating row and mints its `N-<n>` when built, as the catalogue's rule says. **Prior disposition, kept verbatim:** M4 - open; the surface half M8 — **ROUTED TO BOB 2026-09-19 by LED-7 batch 2 (SCHEDULER #2), AND IT IS D-52'S PARENT.** D-52 (an export is recorded but no administrator is notified; routed to BOB by batch 1 for a CHANNEL decision) is one of the ~30 generators this row inventories — its “export notification” sits in the governance class. That fail-closed property is real for ops that reach the compiler and is FALSE for ops that do not: for `list` the failure mode of a missing stamp is unfiltered, which is the opposite of what the comment promises the next reader. SCOPE, stated so this is not over-read: every affected op is member class or above, so this is not a public exposure and not a repeat of D-30 across the two-bucket fence; it is an INTRA-INSTANCE exposure of project existence to a member the project never invited. | D-362 | defect | 2026-09-14 | **A `content_id` HAND-AUTHORED INTO FRONTMATTER UNQUOTED AND CONSISTING ONLY OF DIGITS PARSES AS AN INTEGER, AND `legContentId` THEN ANSWERS `null` — THE LEG SILENTLY BECOMES A WHOLE-DOCUMENT CITATION.** Found by REC-97 while deciding how `op=cite` should WRITE the field, and it is the same class as the defect that item closed, one layer down: `parseScalar` coerces `/^-?\d+$/` to a number before anything reads the value, `legContentId` requires `typeof v === 'string'` and returns null otherwise, and `legHasAuthoredExtent` does not list `content_id` at all — so a leg that named a part reads as a leg that named none, with no finding anywhere. **NOT reachable from any plane writer**: REC-97's `#legExtentLines` writes `content_id: "<id>"` QUOTED for exactly this reason and asserts the quoting on the bytes (`cite-extent.test.mjs` §6), and REC-84's version-leg writer keys off the column rather than the frontmatter. The only route is a `bundle.md` authored by hand or by a future writer that does not quote. **The probability is small and is not the point**: a content id is a sha256 rendered as lowercase hex, so an all-digit one is about `(10/16)^64` ≈ 1e-14 — but the failure mode is a SILENT DROP in the construct whose whole purpose is that the record points at what a member said it points at, and "rare" is not a property a gate can rely on. COSTS while open: nothing measurable today; the exposure begins the moment a second writer of this field exists or a member authors frontmatter directly. CLOSING IT TAKES: one arm in `checkLegExtentGrammar` refusing a `content_id` that is present and not a string (it already refuses a string that is not 64 hex — this is the same rule over the type it was written assuming), plus its C-45 sub-number and its suite arm. | D-401 | defect | 2026-09-17 | **THE STAND-DOWN PROTOCOL RESERVED SESSION CLOSURE TO BOB BY HAND ON A CAPABILITY CLAIM NOBODY HAD TESTED, AND THE CLAIM WAS FALSE.** `kickoffs/BOB.md` and `kickoffs/CONDUCT.md` both said *a session cannot close itself; closing it is the operator's*, and CONDUCT.md went further — **a peer must not do it even having verified it is safe.** Falsified 2026-09-17 by BOB #13 by CALLING THE TOOL instead of re-reading the sentence: `archive_session` accepts a peer's session id, accepts the literal `"self"`, stops the process, and under `bypassPermissions` — this project's default per `CLAUDE.md` — asks nobody. **THE CAPABILITY WAS THERE THE WHOLE TIME; THE PROHIBITION WAS INVENTED.** It is `CLAUDE.md`'s *a blocker is a claim, and nothing here audits one*, and it is **BOB.md's own rule 6 landing on BOB.md a second time** — the *there is no message channel to a running worker* claim was falsified in exactly this way three days earlier, in the same file, and the stand-down step was then written with the same untested premise. **THE MEASURED COST: BOB #12 wrote *this session is ready to be closed* and ran on for hours, holding a 635 MB worktree and LANDING TWO COMMITS TO `main` DURING ITS SUCCESSOR'S SESSION** — two sessions in one lane (DEC-3), and the reason one of them ROWED D-399 while the other FIXED it. **RULED BY BOB THE SAME DAY:** *"BOB #12 being alive is not my problem. The protocol was supposed to be updated so that a lane's retired session was archived without my involvement."* | M0 · **FIXED 2026-09-17 in both kickoffs, and DRIVEN on BOB #12 rather than described.** The act is the SUCCESSOR'S first step, because a retiring session cannot verify its own deadness and the successor already owns the checkout from the click. D-398's three conditions still bind and are re-checked AT THE MOMENT OF ACTING — BOB #12's tree read *uncommitted* forty minutes before it read clean. **Reclamation is TWO acts** (the tool's *cleans up its worktree* is a vendor claim that did not hold): archive, then `git worktree remove`. Measured 5.4 GiB → 6.1 GiB free, 12 worktrees → 11. **RESIDUE, NAMED: no gate asserts any of this.** Nothing in `plancheck` can see a retired-but-running session, and the next successor obeying the new step is the only thing that proves it took. A protocol is not an instrument.  **AND THE ACT CAN BE REFUSED, WHICH D-401 DID NOT KNOW AND THE PROTOCOL DID NOT SAY.** Measured 2026-09-17 by BOB #13 attempting the successor-archive on CONDUCT #2 after verifying all three conditions at the moment of acting — not running, tree clean, tip `1cd5618a` IDENTICAL to `origin/main`: `archive_session` REFUSED with *it still has live work (an agent run, a Remote Control client, a queued message or a background task)*. `ListAgents` read it `idle` and `isRunning` was false, so the cause is the remaining one — **`remoteControlActive: true`, `remoteControlState: "on"`.** **SUPERSEDED BY D-405 THE SAME DAY, AND THE CORRECTION MATTERS: RC WAS ONE OF **TWO** BLOCKERS, AND THE SESSION ITSELF CAN CLEAR IT.** This row originally read *a session connected to Remote Control cannot be archived by its successor, however dead its work is* and concluded a peer is helpless. Both halves were wrong. A wedged tool call was blocking it too, so the single-variable test that appeared to refute RC refuted only its SUFFICIENCY (D-405's 2x2). And the remedy is not an operator act at all: **a session may turn off its OWN Remote Control, so it does that as the last act of standing down** — nobody else's connection is touched, and RC stays on for every live lane. **THE CONSEQUENCE IS RESOURCE, NOT CORRECTNESS: the six agent worktrees CONDUCT #2 holds (~3.8 GiB, every one verified merged, clean and an ancestor) stay locked until it is archived**, and archiving is what releases the lock. **THE REFUSAL IS ALSO THE RIGHT BEHAVIOUR AND IS NOT A BUG TO ROUTE AROUND** — it is the harness refusing to archive something that may be in use, which is exactly the protection that makes an automatic sweep safe. The defect is that D-401's protocol presented the act as always available. *Prior disposition, moved here verbatim at closing (LED-7 batch 10, 2026-09-21):* M0 · **PROTOCOL CORRECTED, CONDITION OUTSTANDING.** `kickoffs/BOB.md` and `kickoffs/CONDUCT.md` now say what to do when the archive is REFUSED: **do not retry in a loop, do not reach for `set_remote_control` on a session you do not own, and do not treat the refusal as failure of the three conditions** — record that the conditions were met, name the blocker, and surface the ONE act only the operator can take (archive it from the sidebar, or disconnect Remote Control from it). `retirable.mjs` is unaffected: it judges whether a session SHOULD be retired; whether the harness will PERMIT it is a separate answer that only the call gives. **OUTSTANDING: CONDUCT #2 is verified retirable and unarchived, holding ~3.8 GiB.** | CLOSED 2026-09-21 IN FACT by LED-7 batch 10 (SCHEDULER #5): the protocol this row corrected is the one every successor now runs: it archives its predecessor under D-398's three conditions re-checked at the moment of acting, then runs `git worktree remove` (`kickoffs/BOB.md`, `kickoffs/CONDUCT.md`, `kickoffs/SCHEDULER.md`), driven today on SCHEDULER #4 (M-81) as on SCHEDULER #3 (M-80). The condition its last disposition held open no longer holds on this machine: CONDUCT #2 predates the 2026-09-19 account switch and is in none of this account's 146 sessions, and none of the clone's 10 worktrees is one of its six (2026-09-21). The judgement half is `tools/retirable.mjs`; a retired session landing after its successor is placed as M0-84. |

The row outlived its remedy by seven weeks. **Prior disposition, kept verbatim:** M4 - open |
| D-187 | design | 2026-08-04 | **A published case was built as exactly ONE inquiry, and nobody ever chose that — it was assumed by every item in the chain.** MEASURED against source when Bob asked for a fact check of his own definition: `store.mjs:3539` refuses verbatim *"publishing publishes ONE case: pass target=<inquiry id>"*; `published` is a state of an INQUIRY (`STATES.inquiry`); `published_bundles` is keyed `(bundle_id, edition)`; `index.mjs:3667` builds the container as `case: body.bundleId` with its own comment *"the parts listed here ARE the bundle's files"*; `MILESTONES.md:428` says *"a concluded inquiry is published"*. Bob's definition is the opposite and is RULED (DEC-44): a case is one or MORE findings, scoped to the project that gathered them. **THE ESCAPE HATCH IS CLOSED BY DEC-32, ruled the same day** — a parent inquiry citing children would collapse several propositions into one conclusion with one falsifier, which is the overclaim DEC-32 exists to prevent; so the two rulings are consistent only if a case is a CONTAINER over findings. Second measured consequence: a published child *NAMES its parent and siblings, serving neither* (UI-18), so even under composition a stranger holding the zip cannot read the findings it points at without returning to the instance — defeating S9's read-without-our-cooperation premise. | D-412 | gap | 2026-09-17 | **THE ESTATE AUDITS EXPOSURE AND NOBODY AUDITS RESIDUE — a worktree can be perfectly healthy, correctly reported `0 EXPOSED`, and never reclaimed by anything.** Raised by CONDUCT #3 from REC-113, which built a pristine checkout in `/tmp` to measure a clean baseline — **correct practice, and the very run that caught the `npm ci` symlink trap** — and it stayed REGISTERED as a git worktree after its agent was gone. **`plancheck` saw it and reported `0 EXPOSED`, which was TRUE: its work was merged, nothing was at risk, nothing was wrong. Nothing asks *is this still needed* — a RESIDUE question, pointed at cost. A tree that is clean, merged and abandoned scores perfectly on the first and is invisible to the second, **so the healthier a residue is the more certainly it is ignored.** **AND THE POPULATION IS WIDER THAN THE INSTRUMENTS LOOK:** `retirable.mjs` keys on SESSIONS and their worktrees; this one had no live session. `strandedwork.mjs` enumerates `git worktree list`, which DID see it — the enumeration was right and no arm asked the second question of it. **A scratch checkout OUTSIDE `.claude/worktrees/` belongs to no lane, no session and no sweep.** | CLOSED 2026-09-21 AS A DEBT ROW by LED-7 batch 10 (SCHEDULER #5) — PLACED as a BACKLOG task under its own id, D-412, after M0-85 with the session-hygiene instruments, its fix named as this row names it: D-398's three conditions asked of a TREE, the over-strictness arm as the item, and NOT folded into `strandedwork`, which still asks only the exposure question (no reclaim or residue arm in `tools/strandedwork.mjs`, 2026-09-21). **Prior disposition, kept verbatim:** M0 · **OPEN, and NOT fixed here.** It is adjacent to `retirable.mjs` (D-402) and to `strandedwork.mjs` but is neither: the predicate would key on a worktree being REGISTERED, CLEAN, MERGED and owned by no live session — the same three conditions D-398 already names, asked of a tree rather than of a session. **The acceptance test is the one this estate keeps needing: it must NOT name a tree a live worker is using**, so its over-strictness arm is the item, exactly as it was for stranded work. **Do not fold it into `strandedwork`** — that arm's whole design argument is that it warns about LOSS, and mixing a cost signal into a loss signal is how both get tuned out. |
| D-144 | defect | 2026-08-01 | **Re-ratifying a bundle DESTROYS the previous attestation — the record can still serve an old published hash and can no longer say who attested it.** VERIFIED at `bio-plane/src/store.mjs:5934-5940`: `published_bundles` is keyed on `bundle_id` alone and the write is `ON CONFLICT(bundle_id) DO UPDATE SET bundle_sha, ratified_at, attestor_key, attestor_member, gate_version, sig_armored` — every field replaced. **ONE CORRECTION TO HOW THIS WAS FIRST REPORTED, and it changes the fix rather than the severity.** The system's headline promise HOLDS: `published_shas` is keyed `(sha256, bundle_id, path)` and is genuinely append-only, so a hash once published stays answerable forever and `op=verify` on an old hash still works — the comment at 5941 saying exactly that is true. What is destroyed is the ATTESTATION PROVENANCE of the earlier publication: who signed it, when, with which key, under which catalogue version. So the record can answer 'yes, these bytes were published' and cannot answer 'by whom, and under what rules' — in a system whose entire product is that claims carry their author, and about the one act it calls irreversible. THE FIX IS NOT IDEMPOTENCE, which is what the first reading would have suggested: re-ratification is legitimate (a bundle revised and re-published SHOULD carry a new attestation). The fix is to make `published_bundles` accumulate like `published_shas` already does — key it `(bundle_id, ratified_at)` or chain each attestation to the one it supersedes — so the history of who stood behind which version is kept rather than replaced. Note a storyboard (`SB-OUTPUT` S16) designs a screen promising exactly that accumulation and cites `published_shas` as its justification without reading the row above it. | CLOSED 2026-09-21 IN FACT by LED-7 batch 11 (SCHEDULER #6): `published_bundles` has been keyed `(bundle_id, edition)` since REC-14 (`1ba5d5d1`, 2026-08-03, an ancestor of `origin/main`), and ratification writes `ON CONFLICT(bundle_id,edition) DO NOTHING`: new bytes take the next edition (`highest + 1`) and different bytes under an existing edition are refused `EDITION_EXISTS` (`store.mjs`, the ratify path, whose comment names this row closed). An earlier edition keeps its own signature, attestor, time and gate version. Driven by `bio-plane/test/publish.test.mjs`, arm *edition 1 KEEPS its own signature, attestor and time after edition 2 lands (D-144 closed as a feature)*, whose negative control reproduces D-144 on purpose. **Prior disposition, kept verbatim:** M6 - open |

| D-199 | design | 2026-08-04 | **The `ai` credential class and its declared task scope — DESIGN AHEAD, recorded here by CONDUCT enacting DEC-55 (Bob, 2026-08-04), because nothing reaches the plane today and a scope kept only in the decision register reaches only the person who already knows it.** Whoever builds the assistant (DEC-27) builds to these five. (1) **Mint the `ai` class in `classify()` on the DEC-37 precedent**, with a DECLARED TASK SCOPE enforced at the gate in `scopeFor`'s shape. (2) **The task scope is declared IN THE RECORD, never in a settings row** — DEC-17's reasoning transplanted: a settings row *"would be a way to change the standard with nothing to read afterwards"*, and what an AI credential may reach is exactly the thing that must be amendable only as an authored, dated, on-the-record act. (4) **The record names the token IDENTITY and the PRINCIPAL behind it** — an organisation-scoped key acts for the group with nobody individual behind it; a member-scoped key is attributable. (5) **THE EXISTING MACHINE FENCES GENERALISE TO THE NEW CLASS BY CONSTRUCTION — AND ONLY BECAUSE OF REC-46, WHICH LANDED THE SAME DAY.** Until then `store.mjs` answered *"is this a machine"* for itself ELEVEN times in two hand-typed shapes while the catalog answered it a third way with a word list that knew nothing of the prefix, and REC-45 had measured the cost: the gate accepted `asserted_by: token:member`. REC-46 collapsed them into one predicate over the `token:<class>` stamp, so **a token stamping `token:ai` is caught by every `MACHINE_CANNOT_*` refusal automatically**. Asked a day earlier this would have meant editing eleven sites and probably missing one. **The negative control is therefore cheap and MANDATORY, and ships WITH the class rather than after it:** mint an `ai`-class credential and assert that `MACHINE_CANNOT_PUBLISH`, `_CONCLUDE`, `_RELEASE`, `_REOPEN`, `_CORRESPOND`, `_MOVE_ACTION` and D-151's resolve/forward refusals each fire BY NAME — and that removing the predicate makes them all pass. **SEQUENCE THE CHECK ROLE FIRST**: DEC-24 calls it *"the role most worth building"* because the threat model is self-directed overclaiming, and it is also the safest, since a role that only reads and raises needs the narrowest scope of the four (EXTRACT, PURSUE, FIND, CHECK). | CLOSED 2026-09-21 IN FACT by LED-7 batch 11 (SCHEDULER #6), the five points walked against the code as batch 5 named, all built by PL-11 / IS-5 (`05951df9`, 2026-08-08): (1) `classify()` admits `ai` (`index.mjs`), its scope enforced by the `aiTaskScope` shape and named in no OPS row; (2) the scope is on the RECORD: `ai_credentials` carries `task_scope`, `scope_writes`, `scope_note`, `minted_by` and `minted_at` (`schema.mjs`), never a settings row; (3) minting is a MEMBER act: `aicredentialmint` and `aicredentialrevoke` admit admin and member, `probe` excluded by name for this reason, and the store refuses a machine stamp (C-29.1); | D-220 | design | 2026-08-06 | **VERSIONS OF ONE DOCUMENT ARE ALREADY RECORDED AND NOTHING EXPOSES THEM, so the code that needs the version chain reconstructs it by full-text search and gets it wrong.** RULED by Bob 2026-08-06: *"Versions of the same document should be linked so that the system recognizes that reality. Each version should also be indexed by the same url (or id that refers back to the url.) These capabilities should be used in code that would benefit to know about this versioning reality."* **THE INDEX HE IS DESCRIBING ALREADY EXISTS** — verified in `schema.mjs` 2026-08-06: `captured_locators` is keyed `(address_norm, capture_sha, via)` with an index on `(address_norm, first_retrieved)`, carrying `first_retrieved`, `last_retrieved` and `observations`; `register` maps `capture_sha` to `bundle_id` and is indexed both ways. **So "every version at this address, in date order, with its bundle" is ONE INDEXED JOIN over two existing tables.** The versioning reality is recorded; no op answers it. **THEREFORE: DO NOT ADD AN EDGE BETWEEN VERSIONS.** An explicit `supersedes` relation would be a SECOND COPY of a fact the record already holds, which is the mirror-and-drift class this project refuses (D-164's solve-it-once; D-138's guard that guarded nothing). The link Bob asks for is the join, EXPOSED. **WHAT THE UNCHANGED CASE ALREADY DOES RIGHT, so it is not re-litigated:** a re-capture of identical content creates no bundle and no second register entry — it advances `last_retrieved` and increments `observations` on the existing row (Bob's own ruling, 2026-07-30, `LINK-FIDELITY.md`). What has no structural form is the CHANGED case, where a new bundle is created and its only tie to its predecessor is a SENTENCE OF PROSE in the body naming the earlier bundle id. **CODE THAT WOULD BENEFIT (Bob's third clause), in the order the benefit is largest:** (1) `heldMatch` in `civicos-ui/app.html`, which today searches `locator:"<url>"` and is the site of D-221; the join replaces the search and removes that defect by construction rather than patching it. (2) The **investigative session** (DEC-60): a run that reads sixty versions of one calendar as sixty documents has a distorted picture of what the record holds, which is the false-coverage hazard `STORE-AS-CACHE.md` names, arriving at the document level. (3) **Search result rendering** — sixty bundles at one address are sixty versions of one document, and a result list presenting them as sixty documents misrepresents the record. (4) **Monitoring** and (5) **`op=audit`'s cross-bundle duplicate sweep**, both of which D-60 records as comparing raw hashes; that line is partly stale (see D-60's correction) and the join gives either one its candidate set directly. (6) **A reader of a citation** — a case cites a specific bundle and that citation must never drift, but the LIVE record can show that later versions exist (a published case cannot be affected, and must not be). **RESEARCHED IN DEPTH 2026-08-06 (sub-session), and the fix is SMALLER than this row assumed — both halves already exist in code.** (i) **The read method exists and was deliberately left unwired:** `Store#capturedLocators({ addressNorm })` (`store.mjs:14779`, REC-26), whose own docstring says *"Deliberately not wired to a control-plane op."* **Its only caller anywhere in the repo is `test/monitor-cadence.test.mjs:415`** — in production nothing reads it. (ii) **The join already exists:** `#conditionBundlesForHost` (`store.mjs:9991`) already runs `captured_locators JOIN register ON r.capture_sha = cl.capture_sha`, scoped by GLOB to a host prefix and used to pick queue-condition subjects. So D-220 is *generalise an existing join and wire an existing method to an op* — not new machinery. (iii) **One write site, and it is unconditional:** `recordCapturedLocator` (`store.mjs:14747`), called only from `op=acquire` (`index.mjs:2938`) for every capture. New bytes at a known address INSERT a row; the same bytes seen again bump `observations` and widen the `MIN`/`MAX` interval — *"Seeing the same bytes again later is not a duplicate, it is the observation that proves the target held still in between."* `via` is in the key deliberately (D-96): an archive sighting of the same bytes is a different fact. Address normalisation is `normalizeAddress` (`subresources.mjs:520`) and is deliberately conservative — it does NOT strip trailing slashes or case-fold the path, because *"a normalisation MISS looks exactly like 'not captured'."* **CORRECTION TO THIS ROW'S OWN CONSUMER LIST:** consumer (5) named `op=audit`'s *cross-bundle duplicate sweep*. **No such sweep exists.** `auditPass` iterates bundles and calls `checkBundle` per bundle; `checkRegisterIntegrity` (`bio-checks.mjs:3536`) buckets within one bundle's `provenance.json` only. The phrase was inherited from D-60 and is a category error — there is nothing cross-bundle to move onto the join, which makes the join the thing that would CREATE that capability rather than improve it. **A CONSUMER THE ROW MISSED, and it is the largest one:** **MONITORING SELECTS PER BUNDLE, NEVER PER ADDRESS** (`store.mjs:15983` `#monitorCadencePlan`, `:16023` `#monitorCadenceTick`, keyed on `bundles.monitor_enabled/monitor_frequency/monitor_last_checked`). So sixty versions of one calendar are **sixty independent monitor subjects** — the same address fetched sixty times on sixty schedules. Monitoring an ADDRESS is what a member means; monitoring a bundle is what the code does. | CLOSED 2026-09-21 AS A DEBT ROW by LED-7 batch 12 (SCHEDULER #6), PLACED as a BACKLOG task under its own id, D-220, after D-84, NARROWED to consumer (3). BUILT, verified at the code: `op=versionchain` (PL-10, `index.mjs`; `Store#versionChain`), the join exposed with no edge added, as ruled; consumer (1) `heldMatch` (D-221, closed); consumer (4) the UI (`civicos-ui/app.html` reads `versionchain`). NOT BUILT: consumer (3), the investigative session, which `INVESTIGATIVE-SESSION.md` §"What the session sees" requires to read versions as versions (`agent-worker/src` calls nine plane ops, `versionchain` not among them). Consumer (2), monitoring per address, is left UNJUDGED on the row for its builder. **Prior disposition, kept verbatim:** M3 · open (Bob 2026-08-06) — design settled, no schema change, and the implementation is smaller than assumed: **generalise `#conditionBundlesForHost`'s join, wire `capturedLocators` to an op**, then consumers — (1) `heldMatch` (closes D-221), (2) monitoring per address, (3) the investigative session, (4) search rendering |
| D-394 | gap | 2026-09-17 | **A REFRESHED DOCUMENT PRODUCES NEW CONTENT ROWS THAT ARE RELATED TO THE OLD ONES BY NOTHING, SO A MEMBER WHOSE CASE RESTS ON A PASSAGE IS NEVER TOLD A NEWER VERSION OF IT EXISTS.** Raised by Bob's question of 2026-09-17 — *is content updated when a refreshed version of a document is obtained* — and the answer separates into two cases that look like one. **CASE 1, RE-EXTRACTION (the SAME bytes under a better engine): BUILT and correct.** The content row's identity includes the extraction CHAIN, so a re-read yields a new row for the same extent; `REC-82`'s writer carries the prior referent forward when target and extent match, marks it `stale`, and leaves the move to a member's act, with the control that proves it can fail in the suite. D-252 records what that narrowly avoided: `inquiry_basis` is delete-then-insert re-projected on every promotion, so a naive projection would have SILENTLY RE-POINTED every authored citation the first time anything re-read the document — the move Bob ruled the record never makes (5.8). **CASE 2, A REFRESHED DOCUMENT (NEW bytes, a new `capture_sha`): NOT BUILT, AND NOT ROWED UNTIL NOW.** The new capture IS extracted and indexed — `promote` writes its `capture_text` and its content rows — so content is CREATED for the new version. **What does not exist is any relation between the new rows and the old.** `BIO_Content_Framework_v0_10.md` §18 rules the doctrine: an authored edge is never re-pointed to a newer capture without a member's act, the content row's identity includes the capture, a new capture is a new row, and *"the same passage across versions" is a derived relation between rows, PROPOSED AND NEVER APPLIED*. **The never-applied half is ruled and right; the PROPOSED half is designed nowhere and built nowhere** — driven 2026-09-17: ZERO occurrences of any cross-capture content relation in `store.mjs`, and no row or debt entry in the queue or this file (the only `re-point` hits are D-252's chain case). **THE CONSEQUENCE IS A TRUSTWORTHINESS GAP RATHER THAN AN OVERCLAIM, WHICH IS WHY NO EXISTING INSTRUMENT CATCHES IT:** the member's citation stays honestly pointed at the capture it was made against, every grade stays earned, nothing claims more than it can support — and a case may rest on a passage the publisher has since revised while the record holds BOTH captures and says nothing. Every gate here is pointed at the record claiming too much; this is the record knowing something and not telling anyone. **THE SUBSTRATE EXISTS**, which is what makes this a gap and not a research question: `op=versionchain` already answers for one document address, and captures carry their shas. **WHAT CLOSING IT TAKES:** a design decision first, and it is doctrine-adjacent enough to name here rather than assume — WHEN the correspondence is derived (at capture of the newer version, or lazily at read), WHAT it is attached to (the content row, the edge, or a read-time answer), and HOW it is shown so that a proposal is never mistaken for a re-pointing. The extent-match test `REC-82` already uses for the chain case is the obvious candidate and is NOT obviously right across versions, where a revision may move a passage between pages. Relation: `BIO_Content_Framework_v0_10.md` §18 piece 1, D-164, D-252, `REC-82`. **Prior disposition, moved here verbatim at LED-7 batch 12 because it carries a residue marker:** M4 · **DESIGNED 2026-09-17 — `BIO_Content_Framework_v0_10.md` §18.1, which is the home that already ruled the doctrine. The three decisions this row named are made.** **WHEN: lazily at READ, never at capture** — a correspondence derived at capture is a STORED CLAIM and goes stale the moment either extraction improves, which is this corpus's most-repeated failure; a read-time answer cannot go stale and costs nothing for the refreshed captures nobody cites. **WHAT: a read-time answer about a PAIR, attached to nothing persistent.** Not the content row (§14.4 puts the capture inside its identity) and not the edge (an edge gaining a pointer to a newer capture IS the re-pointing). **Attaching it to nothing is the MECHANISM rather than an omission: the strongest guarantee a proposal is never mistaken for a re-pointing is to make it impossible to persist one** — it cannot then be followed by accident, re-projected on promotion, or inherited as fact. **HOW: a notice on the citation in three states, and the third is the design.** This row was right that `REC-82`'s extent test is not obviously right across versions, so extent-match is admitted as a signal for a CANDIDATE and never as evidence of identity; where it fails the answer is **UNDETERMINED**, not a guess. **THE DECOMPOSITION THAT MADE IT TRACTABLE: the valuable half does not need the hard half.** The trustworthiness gap is *never told a newer version exists*, which `op=versionchain` can answer TODAY with certainty; WHICH PASSAGE survives is separate, harder and worth much less. **Collapsing the two is what kept both unbuilt.** And undetermined is nearly the whole value here — *a newer version exists and I cannot tell you whether your passage is in it* is exactly what sends a member to look, which is the act the doctrine reserves to them. **STILL OPEN:** nothing is BUILT and no item is rowed — the design is deliberately handed over without a decomposition, because the split above changes what the items are. **And one question is explicitly NOT settled: whether the notice reaches a member whose case is already PUBLISHED, and through what surface, which is a publication question rather than a content one.** | CLOSED 2026-09-21 AS A DEBT ROW by LED-7 batch 12 (SCHEDULER #6), PLACED as a BACKLOG task under its own id, D-394, after REC-122 with the M4 product rows: DESIGNED 2026-09-17 in `BIO_Content_Framework_v0_10.md` §18.1 and NOT BUILT (its only occurrence in `bio-plane/src` is a comment naming it as a different act with its own design). Its substrate is built: `op=versionchain` (PL-10) and REC-82's carry. |
| D-389 | defect | 2026-09-16 | **`truncated: false` RESTS ON THE OVER-FETCH BEING WIDE ENOUGH TO ABSORB THE FENCE, AND ON A FULL RAW FETCH IT IS A COVERAGE CLAIM THE READER CANNOT SUPPORT. All THREE arms of `op=frontier` share it.** Raised by REC-109 while closing D-385, and raised rather than fixed for a stated reason: it is a property of the OVER-FETCH MECHANISM, which the document arm (REC-103), the content arm (REC-109) and the meaning arm (REC-95/CONDUCT #11) all use, so fixing it in one arm of three is the mirror-and-drift class in the one place it would be most dangerous. **THE SHAPE.** Each arm fetches `R` raw rows (`(cap + 1) * 2` at document and content, `(cap + 1) * 3` at meaning), gates them, then cuts at `cap`. When the raw fetch comes back SHORT of `R` the supply is exhausted, the gated list is complete, and `truncated` is sound in both directions. **When it comes back FULL, rows beyond it were never fetched and their visibility is unknown — so a gated list of `cap` or fewer is published with `truncated: false`, which claims a completeness the method did not establish.** It is unsound only when the fence drops more than half a full over-fetched page (a third, at meaning), which is why the factor is doing real work and not decoration. **WHAT CLOSING IT TAKES, and the analysis is done so the next owner does not re-derive it.** The sound form adds one disjunct: `raw.length === R`, i.e. *the supply was not exhausted, so do not read this as complete*. **AND IT LEAKS NOTHING, which was checked rather than assumed** — on a full raw fetch a viewer entitled to every row ALSO has `page.length > cap` and so ALSO reads `true`, so the new disjunct never distinguishes the withheld viewer from the entitled one; on an exhausted fetch it is false for everybody and the flag falls back to each caller's own list. The cost is that `truncated` reads `true` on any instance whose supply exceeds the over-fetch, which is the fail-safe direction for a coverage claim and is what a cursor-less paged read is entitled to say. **Whether that is acceptable across all three arms at once is the decision, and it is a disclosure-adjacent read change (I3) rather than an implementation choice.** | CLOSED 2026-09-21 AS A DEBT ROW by LED-7 batch 13 (SCHEDULER #6), PLACED as a BACKLOG task under its own id, D-389, after CAP-13, its fix as this row analysed it: one disjunct, `raw.length === R`, in the over-fetch all three arms share, leaking nothing. The acceptance question it left open is answered by CLAUDE.md §2 (a coverage claim the method did not establish is not made), stated on the backlog row. **Prior disposition, kept verbatim:** M3 · RECORD · open — raised by REC-109 2026-09-16, region-bounded and reported rather than absorbed. PINNED so the next reader meets the decision rather than the defect: `observation-content.test.mjs` arm G5 names this row. Wants a queue row spanning all three arms. |
| D-351 | gap | 2026-09-14 | **A GOOGLE DRIVE EXPORT IS NOT BYTE-STABLE, SO `capture_sha` — THE TRUST ROOT — DIFFERS ON EVERY RE-FETCH OF A DOCUMENT THAT DID NOT CHANGE, AND THREE MECHANISMS THAT KEY ON IT GO QUIET OR CRY WOLF.** MEASURED by CAP-8 (2026-09-14, MEASUREMENTS.md §4): three consecutive exports of one unchanged document, ~1.5 s apart, through the plane's egress, produced **three distinct `capture_sha` in three takes** for both `.odt` and `.ods`. Every export is Google's CONVERSION AT FETCH TIME and the conversion is not reproducible — the ZIP envelope differs every request in both kinds, and for `.odt` even `content.xml` differs by one character (per-request automatic-style names). For `.ods` the envelope alone moves and `content.xml` is byte-identical across all three, which is the shape of the fix. COSTS, all three in the direction of a record that says LESS than it could rather than more, which is the safe direction and is why this is a gap and not a defect: (1) **C-18.3's corroboration fold can never fire on a Drive document** — its RAW arm compares `capture_sha` and two captures of one Doc will never match, so the record holds two review items where there is one; (2) **the NORMALISED arm cannot rescue it either**, because `profile.digests.determined` requires the bytes to have been read AS TEXT with a CERTAIN stack and an ODF container is never read as text, so a Drive capture records `evidentiary: null` and the sweep correctly refuses to treat two nulls as equal; (3) **`resolveLinks`' identity BRACKET can never fire** — its strongest arm is one set of bytes observed on both sides of an interval hashing equal, which is unreachable here, so every Drive link falls to `undetermined` and a monitoring tick would report a CHANGE on every single re-fetch. CLOSING IT TAKES: an evidentiary digest for the office/ODF path that normalises over the CONTAINER rather than over decoded text — `content.xml` is the obvious substrate and the `.ods` measurement shows it is stable — which is an I1 §4c change (`digests.determined` currently gated on `profiled_from_text`) plus one producer in the acquire path; the `.odt` per-request style names need normalising out and that is a measurement before it is a build. | CLOSED 2026-09-21 AS A DEBT ROW by LED-7 batch 13 (SCHEDULER #6), PLACED as a BACKLOG task under its own id, D-351, after CAP-11, which calibrates the same export step and cites this row's measurement. Its sizing condition is met: CAP-7 counted the population (MEASUREMENTS.md M-13, 2026-09-14: 50 Drive links, 22 distinct targets, 16 documents in COFF-6's whole census), small, and every cost in the direction of a record that says LESS than it could. The fix is this row's own CLOSING IT TAKES: an evidentiary digest over the ODF container, with `.odt`'s style names a measurement before a build. **Prior disposition, kept verbatim:** M2 · open — CAPTURE's, and NOT in CAP-8's scope (its row rules on recognition and the hop, not on dedup) and deliberately not smuggled in; sized as one item for CAPTURE once CAP-7's census says how much Drive material there actually is |

CLOSING IT TAKES: one per-pair fact on `#affordanceFacts` (`#isProjectOwner(target, viewer)`, the D-310 pattern), seven `applies` predicates derived one at a time from the refusal each op actually raises, an IC row for the ADDITION, and a decision on whether a machine credential's act set narrows at all. | CLOSED 2026-09-21 AS A DEBT ROW by LED-7 batch 14 (SCHEDULER #6), PLACED as a BACKLOG task under its own id, D-311, after REC-158, carrying BOTH halves this row holds: the seven roster acts folded in by the fix this row names (one per-pair fact, `#isProjectOwner(target, viewer)`, and seven `applies` predicates, each from its own refusal), and the machine fence it carried so it would not be lost (`op=affordances` offers `publish` to a credential the store refuses `MACHINE_CANNOT_PUBLISH`). FW-7 (`2d11c03e`, 2026-07-31) built the recognisers that resolve a reference to a registry entity and grade the METHOD: `resolutions` keeps `entity_id`, `grade`, `method` and `basis` per (capture, ref) (`schema.mjs`), driven by `bio-plane/test/resolution.test.mjs`; construct 6 renders graded resolutions BUILT. | D-107 | gap | 2026-07-31 | **The installer has no scripted deploy, and that is part of why D-106 went unnoticed for thirteen releases.** `newgroup/DEPLOY.md` documents deployment as: open the dashboard, select all in the editor, delete, paste `dist/newgroup.bundled.mjs`, Deploy. A manual paste produces no record of what was deployed, no read-back verification, and no place for a version assertion to live, which is exactly the blind spot the plane does NOT have: `bio-plane/scripts/deploy.mjs` refuses to report success until it reads the script back from the account and hashes it against the signed asset. This session deployed the installer with `wrangler deploy` and verified by reading the script back through the API (version, absence of the old version, and `bindings: []`), which worked but is not written down anywhere. The installer deserves the plane's discipline: a script that deploys, reads back, and asserts both the embedded `RELEASE_VERSION` and the empty binding set, since the no-bindings property is a structural security guarantee that a hand-paste into the wrong Worker could silently break. Not urgent while installer releases are rare, but the cost of the manual path is now measured rather than assumed.  REINFORCED 2026-07-31 by a finding that makes this sharper than when it was written: a fresh checkout on Bob's machine was authenticated to a DIFFERENT Cloudflare account (`neologic`, `2f4cabcc...`) than the project's, because `wrangler` takes its identity from an OAuth session stored on the machine and a machine is often logged in for unrelated work. An installer deploy from that state would have SUCCEEDED and put the installer in the wrong account with no error to notice, which is precisely the class of silent wrong outcome the plane's own deploy path refuses to permit. PARTIAL FIX SHIPPED: both wrangler.jsonc files now pin `account_id`, so the repository rather than the machine decides where a Worker goes. `scripts/deploy.mjs` was never exposed, because it takes the account from CF_ACCT and talks to the REST API directly; the gap was always the installer's `wrangler deploy`. The rest of this item still stands, and the account discovery is an argument for it: the installer deserves the plane's discipline, meaning a script that deploys, reads the Worker back, and asserts the embedded RELEASE_VERSION, the empty binding set, AND the account it actually landed in. | CLOSED 2026-09-21 AS A DEBT ROW by LED-7 batch 15 (SCHEDULER #6), PLACED as a BACKLOG task under its own id, D-107, after LED-9, its defect verified present at the code: `newgroup/DEPLOY.md` documents a dashboard paste and `newgroup/scripts/` holds only `embed-release.mjs`, while DIST's law reads the installer back by hand at every cut (`kickoffs/DIST.md` step 9). Its fix is `deploy.mjs`'s read-back pattern applied to the installer, as this row states. **Prior disposition, kept verbatim:** M7 · open |

| D-80 | design | 2026-07-30 | **Contact between aspirations is not detected, and contradiction is a thing to FIND rather than prevent.** RULED by Bob: contradicting aspirations are welcomed, because we may not realise that they contradict and because we learn from trying to achieve aspirations whether they are achieved or not. That removes the arbiter from the design deliberately rather than leaving it unfinished: no precedence between group, project and member scopes, and no forced resolution, since forcing one would suppress exactly the discovery the ruling is pointing at. What remains to build is detection of CONTACT, not of contradiction, because judging whether two prose commitments contradict is not something this framework can establish and inventing that verdict would violate invariant 5. Contact IS establishable: two aspirations directing work at the same entities, touching the same progressions, or ordering the same queue differently. What counts as contact is named in framework §12.1 and not specified, and the surface that shows it does not exist. | M4 · CLOSED 2026-09-21 by BOB #22, BY THE THIRD DOOR — a stated deferral in its home design. `BIO_Content_Framework_v0_10.md`'s front matter now carries an Incomplete bullet naming §12.1: CONTACT is named and NOT SPECIFIED, deferred to the design act that makes an aspiration an object (`node tools/status.mjs 8.goals` ABSENT, re-read 2026-09-21), of which its specification is a required clause; Bob's ruling (no arbiter; a contradiction is found, never prevented) stands. That is SCHEDULER (#5)'s fold 2. **Prior disposition, kept:** open — **ROUTED TO BOB 2026-09-19 by LED-7 batch 2 (SCHEDULER #2).** The row states its own blocker: framework §12.1 NAMES what counts as CONTACT and does not specify it, and the surface that would show it does not exist. No fix can be named until the specification is, so this is not placed. The ruling that removed the arbiter is Bob's and is not in question; what is owed is the specification of contact. **RE-DISPOSED 2026-09-21 by BOB #19: DEFERRED TO THE ACT THAT MAKES AN ASPIRATION AN OBJECT. Nothing on this row falls to BOB before then.** Contact is a relation BETWEEN aspirations, and an aspiration is not an object in this plane: `node tools/status.mjs 8.goals` reads ABSENT (*goals and aspirations as objects*, probe `none of /aspiration/`). Specifying how two objects that do not exist come into contact would design a dependent ahead of its substrate (`CLAUDE.md` §2). So the specification of CONTACT is a required clause of 8.goals' own design act, covering the framework §12.1 shapes: the same entities, the same progressions, and the same queue ordered differently. This row closes into that act when it is placed. Bob's ruling (no arbiter; contradiction is found, not prevented) stands and is not reopened. **SENT 2026-09-21 by SCHEDULER #5 (LED-7) to BOB #20 — this row leaves by the THIRD door once its deferral is stated in its home.** Today the deferral lives only on this row: `BIO_Content_Framework_v0_10.md` §12.1 names CONTACT (the same entities, the same progressions, the same queue ordered differently), and neither that section nor the document's Incomplete sections says it is unspecified or deferred to 8.goals' design act (no `D-80` anywhere in the file, 2026-09-21). | D-145 | design | 2026-08-01 | **Bundle ids are per-instance sequential, so nothing addressed by id survives leaving the instance that minted it.** VERIFIED: `allocId` (`store.mjs:4401`) mints per-instance sequence numbers, so every sovereign instance independently produces `INFO-2026-0001`. Hashes are global and ids are not. This is a hole under M6's whole promise — a record that can be mirrored, verified and outlived by tooling that already exists — because everything the record says ABOUT a document is addressed by id: `references[].target`, `refs`, `inquiry_basis`, `elevated_into`, every citation edge. An export can be verified byte for byte and its internal graph cannot be re-pointed at anything outside itself, and two instances citing each other would collide on the first id either had ever minted. The design pass asked what GRADE a cross-instance citation carries (`AUDIENCES` row 17) and nobody asked whether it can be ADDRESSED at all. Candidate shapes, none free: an instance-scoped prefix (cheap, changes every id and this record is append-only), a UUID beside the human id (no migration, two identifiers for one thing), or citation by content hash with the id as a comment — which is the shape the record's own doctrine already prefers, since identity is canonical and the address is a comment. | M6 · CLOSED 2026-09-21 by LED-7 (SCHEDULER #8), BY THE THIRD DOOR — a stated deferral in its home design, on BOB #23's ruling of SCHEDULER #7's Q1 (`43cd0caf`). `BIO_State_Rules_Consistency_v1_5.md`'s front matter now carries an Incomplete bullet for §1.2: an id is unique within ONE instance, so nothing addressed by id survives leaving it and two instances' ids collide; cross-instance addressing is M6's (`MILESTONES.md`, whose M6 table names this row), designed WITH D-99's WARC and Memento interchange because an export carries an id-addressed graph, and no shape is chosen until M6 is taken up. STILL OPEN: WARC import and export as INTERCHANGE (the internal store stays content-addressed), and a Memento interface. The verdict value is defensible; the BASIS is false, and because the UI shows the plane's basis in the plane's own words rather than paraphrasing it, a member reads a fabricated claim about a source. The fix belongs in the plane, not the viewer: `resolveLinks` should recognise `pick.capture_sha === sourceCapture` and report a self-reference, which is a fourth basis rather than a fourth verdict. | D-60 | design | 2026-07-30 | **A capture has no STABLE DIGEST, so change detection, duplicate detection and contemporaneity all run on a hash that moves for reasons that are not changes.** MEASURED 2026-07-30, two fetches of `oakland.legistar.com/Calendar.aspx` three seconds apart: identical length, **114,177 differing bytes, 31% of the document**, every differing byte inside exactly two hidden fields, `__VIEWSTATE` (115,096 bytes) and `__EVENTVALIDATION` (876 bytes). Normalise those two and the remaining **252,948 bytes, 68.6%, are byte-identical**. Nothing about the calendar changed. ASP.NET reserialises its control tree and reissues its anti-forgery list on every response, and municipal publishing runs on this class of software. Three separate mechanisms are wrong because of it: **monitoring** reports a change on every tick and therefore reports nothing; **duplicate detection** does not fire on re-captures of the pages that get re-captured most, so the record grows a second bundle for one document while C-18.3 and `op=audit` both stay silent because the hashes genuinely differ; and **contemporaneity's strongest arm** can never fire (see D-59). The fix is one thing: beside its raw identity hash, a capture carries a derived digest computed with known-volatile regions normalised to a placeholder. Identity stays raw and raw bytes are never rewritten; comparison uses the stable digest. Volatile regions are RECORDED with their field names, offsets and lengths, and the fact that two fetches differed only there is itself a dated observation worth keeping. Classification is per-family, heuristic, and reclassifiable exactly as chrome detection is, discovered by measurement rather than declared. A volatile region is never treated as evidence. Design written into LINK-FIDELITY.md. **PARTLY DONE 2026-07-30:** the volatile machinery is NOT at `civicos-ui/volatile.mjs` (that path does not exist; the row was wrong). It lives in `docprofile/index.mjs` (the `digests` and `compare` exports) with per-family handlers in `docprofile/handlers/` (`aspnet-webforms.mjs` is the Legistar/viewstate case), validated both directions against two live captures of one Legistar page. The PLANE has not adopted it: monitoring, `op=audit`'s duplicate sweep, and `resolveLinks`' bracket arm all still compare raw hashes, so the plane should import `digests`/`compare` rather than grow a second copy. **ADOPTION FEASIBILITY MEASURED 2026-07-30:** docprofile is 37KB of pure JS, zero external deps, zero node builtins, only internal cross-imports. Bundled through the plane's own esbuild target it tree-shakes to **5.3KB** against 2.4MB of headroom under the 3MB Free limit, so the bundle-size concern that applies to unpdf (D-91) does NOT apply here. docprofile sits at repo root OUTSIDE `bio-plane/`; the import path from `src/` is `../../docprofile/index.mjs`, and because `build` inlines all imports into one self-contained `dist/bio-plane.bundled.mjs`, the deployed artifact and the installer are unaffected by the cross-directory location as long as `docprofile/` is present at BUILD time (it is, in a clean checkout). | D-63 | gap | 2026-07-30 | **Only three stacks have been measured, and every unmeasured one is a handler waiting for evidence.** `docprofile/` now has a structure for recognising a document's stack and kind and deciding what is evidentiary, what is furniture and what is per-render machinery, and it is validated against ASP.NET WebForms (Legistar), WordPress (Oaklandside) and a client-rendered app (OpenGov). | D-38 | design | 2026-07-25 | **The number of Information records a Project can cite is bounded by INLINE_MAX, not by SELECTION_MAX_ITEMS, and the two were set independently.** Citing writes one frontmatter entry per cited record into a single `bundle.md`, measured at 83 bytes per edge at a 25-character bundle id. The enumeration cap is 10,000 and the inline file limit is 1MB, so a MAXIMUM LEGAL SELECTION originally produced a 1,070,846-byte document and was refused by `promote` with `OVERSIZE_INLINE`: an error about inline byte storage, in answer to an operator who selected a legal number of records and pressed cite. Found by `test/cite-scale.mjs`, not by the suite. Two changes in 0.18.0 make it survivable rather than resolved: the Session Log entry is bounded at 20 named ids plus a count (it had listed every id, costing a further 24 bytes per edge), which took an edge from 107 to 84 bytes and lets a maximum 10,000 selection fit at 841KB; and `CITATION_TOO_LARGE` now refuses before anything is written, naming the real limit and the room remaining. The ceiling is now reachable only CUMULATIVELY, and is: at 12,000 edges a further 2,000 refuses and leaves the document byte-identical. `bundle.md` cannot spill to R2 to escape this, because the gate compares it byte-wise against history (C-5, C-12) and `schema.mjs` keeps every file the gate reads inline by rule. Bob's decision, 2026-07-25: leave the ceiling where it is. The tested ceiling is far above any plausible group, lowering the selection cap would penalise selections that are not for citing, and there may be Cloudflare configuration raising these limits that has not been explored. | D-55 | design | 2026-07-29 | **A rendered capture may contain content whose authority is not the hosting site, and `capture.authority` holds exactly one value.** Bob's ruling: if content produced by a third-party script is ever evidence, it must be recorded as produced by that third party and not by the hosting site. The provenance document has one `authority` per capture, so a rendered document carrying an advertiser's, an analytics vendor's, or a CDN-hosted widget's output cannot currently attribute it. Attribution has to be per-origin and sub-document, which is a shape the register has never held: `references[]` targets a bundle and nothing finer, the same granularity problem D-53 is blocked on. Deciding this is a precondition for treating any rendered capture as evidence, not a follow-on. *Prior disposition, kept as the record, VERBATIM:* DOCTRINE (Bob) · BLOCKS D-64 · open, blocks CLIENT-RENDERED.md — **CONFIRMED BOB'S 2026-09-19 by LED-7 batch 2 (SCHEDULER #2), not placeable.** The row's own state already says DOCTRINE (Bob), and nothing in this lane can move it: per-origin, sub-document attribution is a SHAPE the register has never held, and deciding it is a precondition for treating a rendered capture as evidence rather than a follow-on. **— THAT CLAUSE IS THE ROW'S OWN CLAIM, REPEATED HERE BY SCHEDULER #2 WITHOUT MEASURING IT, AND IT WAS FALSE WHEN I WROTE IT.** See BOB #17's narrowing below, read at the schema. CLAUDE.md §5 — a blocker is a claim about the day it was WRITTEN — binds a DISPOSITION that inherits one exactly as it binds the row; a triage that forwards a premise unverified has not triaged it. Left visible rather than edited away, because the sequence is the lesson. Left where it is, and named to BOB with the other doctrine rows of this batch. **NARROWED 2026-09-19 by BOB #17 — THE ROW'S PREMISE IS MATERIALLY STALE AND THE DESIGN IS FAR SMALLER THAN IT SAYS.** This row was written 2026-07-29 and claims per-origin, sub-document attribution is *"a shape the register has never held"*. Read at the schema today, the register holds all three pieces: **per-ORIGIN** — `site_assets` carries `host` and `address` per asset; **the JOIN to the document** — `site_asset_refs` keys `(host, address_norm, primary_sha)` with its own `sha256`, so the record already says WHICH BYTES, FROM WHICH HOST, WERE USED BY WHICH CAPTURE; **sub-DOCUMENT** — the `content` object addresses a part of a document across eight extent kinds, content-addressed over capture_sha + extent + chain, each row carrying its own `chain` and `derivation_cap` (none of which existed when this row was written). So the DATA is not missing and no new register shape is owed. **What is actually missing is narrow and is the whole design:** (1) nothing carries an AUTHORITY value at asset or content grain — `content` has no authority column and inherits the capture's trust root through `capture_sha`; and (2) no rule says a content row over third-party bytes takes THAT origin's authority rather than the hosting site's, which is Bob's ruling restated as a mechanism. **THE ONE LINK NOT TRACED, and the design must check it first rather than assume it:** whether a content row can be minted over subresource bytes at all — the `image` extent arm with `cited_as='bytes'` is the candidate — and whether that row's bytes join to a `site_asset_refs` row. If they do not join, that join is the item and the attribution rests on it. STILL BOB's, still not placeable, but it is now a bounded design act rather than a new shape for the register. **SENT 2026-09-21 by SCHEDULER #8 to BOB #23, Q4 of a group of four (DELEGATION 2026-09-21 SCHEDULER (#8) -> BOB)**, re-verified at the code on `19a0efb6`. | CLOSED 2026-09-21 BY DESIGN (BOB #24). The one untraced link was traced at the code (`c05d71c8`): a content row's document is always a registered capture; the `image` arm's `{part}` form names a subresource's bytes, or any other hash, on a capture that is not an office container and mints UNJOINED, a defect now minted as D-440; and no third party's script output can enter a capture today (`fetchPolicy` refuses every script, image and media reference whose origin is third-party, and nothing renders). The design is in `CLIENT-RENDERED.md` ("DESIGNED 2026-09-21") and is folded into `AUTHORITY-AND-TRUST.md` §What D-55 becomes. Authority stays at DOCUMENT grain. A third party's output that is evidence is ACQUIRED as its own document under the three-valued rule. A rendered capture's authority follows the data: `determined` as the host only when the host alone supplied data and ran code, otherwise `undetermined` with a dated basis naming each other origin. Per-origin attribution of a rendered page's regions is DEFERRED as a refinement that corrects no record. D-64 no longer waits on this row; its build carries the rule. |

| D-56 | unverified | 2026-07-29 | **CPU headroom: MEASURED 2026-07-29, and the worry was unfounded on the numbers available. Measured on biosmoke7 (Workers Free) at 0.44.0: `op=cpuprobe` completed 20 steps of 2,000,000 reference iterations, 40,000,000 in total, and the isolate was killed during step 21 with HTTP 503 `error code: 1102`. Real captures are nowhere near it: the heaviest measured, a news front page, does 49 compute calls over 16.97MB of hashing and parsing. **A SEPARATE AND MORE IMPORTANT FINDING came out of trying: a Worker CANNOT TIME ITSELF.** Cloudflare freezes `Date.now()` during synchronous execution as a timing-attack defence, so the first meter reported zero for every segment of every real capture. Any millisecond figure reported from inside a Worker is a fabrication. Consumption is therefore counted in WORK (calls, bytes) and the ceiling in REFERENCE ITERATIONS; see `src/cpu.mjs`. **WHY THIS STAYS OPEN.** A CPU overrun terminates the isolate: there is no catchable error, no chance to record anything, and the caller sees an opaque 1102. Unlike the subrequest ceiling, this failure mode can never announce itself, so a future scope of work can begin silently overrunning it and the only symptom will be requests that stop working. Nothing is asked of any session by this row. It exists so that an unexplained 1102, or a capture that fails only on large inputs, is read as a CPU overrun on the first guess instead of the fifth. If work volume per capture ever grows by an order of magnitude, re-run `op=cpuprobe` and compare. | CLOSED 2026-09-21 BY THE THIRD DOOR by LED-7 (SCHEDULER #9) — the watch is stated in its home, `CLIENT-RENDERED.md` (§What Workers Paid actually buys), BOB #24's answer to SCHEDULER #9's Q2 (`26de0439`): the 2026-07-29 `op=cpuprobe` measurement (40,000,000 reference iterations, killed at step 21 with 1102), the finding that a Worker cannot time itself (`src/cpu.mjs`), and the rule that an unexplained 1102 is read as a CPU overrun first. | D-123 | gap | 2026-07-31 | **I2's element reference is PDF-shaped and office formats need a per-container form, which is `INTERFACE-CHANGES.md`'s first real use.** I2 records `source: {page, rect} | null`. A genuine fork the proposal must settle: whether the canonical reference is a STRUCTURED union or a per-container STRING (`"Sheet1!B14"`, `"slide7#shape3"`, `"p7#rect(...)"`) with structured fields beside it. ONE WRINKLE WORTH KNOWING BEFORE PROPOSING: FRAMEWORK owns I2, is dormant, and has never consumed it, so nothing breaks TODAY; the whole reason to use the protocol is that registering an interface means not changing it casually later. CONDUCT answers on FRAMEWORK's behalf IN WRITING (protocol step 3), and that answer must be recorded as CONDUCT answering for a dormant area rather than as FRAMEWORK agreeing — the distinction matters when FRAMEWORK wakes and disagrees. Text extraction is the expensive half (glyph-to-Unicode through embedded CMaps) and Bob is right that libraries solve it: `unpdf` is a serverless pdf.js build targeting Workers. | D-90 | doctrine | 2026-07-30 | **Measure decay is loud and never blocking, which is the general rule that derived things inform and authored acts bind.** RULED by Bob, by pointing at the principle already written: the measure never edits the statement, so a measure that may not edit a statement certainly may not block work resting on one. Now invariant 8 of the framework, and it runs through more than bias: an assistant's proposal, a connection grade, a recogniser's confidence all report and none decides. The asymmetry with bias debt is exact rather than arbitrary. Bias debt blocks because the GROUP changed the lens by an authored act and the block is what makes that amendment mean something; a decayed measure means the WORLD changed and nobody decided anything, and an institution improving its behaviour must not be able to freeze a group's in-flight work. The path from decay to a block runs through a person: the measure reports, a member amends or retires the statement, that amendment is authored, and it generates bias debt in the ordinary way. | D-161 | gap | 2026-08-03 | **A connection is anchored to specific passages and then throws the anchor away.** `resolutions` is keyed `(capture_sha, ref, entity_id)` — a SPECIFIC reference inside a capture, with its own grade and method — and `reading_refs` gives each `ref` a kind, key and label. `deriveConnections` (`store.mjs:3499`) then *"collapses them to the STRONGEST grade each capture resolved to the entity at, and forms one connection per PAIR of distinct captures"*, so the connection row carries `a_capture_sha`/`b_capture_sha` and no passage. **Consequence:** following a connection lands a reader on a whole document — a 300-page budget PDF — rather than on the line that grounds it, and a case's citations cannot be checked at the point they are made. (2) **The design question is ANSWERED by Bob and the answer was NOT BUILT.** His 2026-09-14 second pass on 5.4 (`b41db4fb`, 16:39, two and a half hours BEFORE FW-17 landed at 19:05) ruled that the connection pair is the ON-POINT pair, CHOSEN, not merely the strongest-graded mention. FW-17 built exactly the recommendation above instead: `Store.deriveConnections` keeps, per capture, the row that wins `GRADE_RANK[r.grade] > GRADE_RANK[cur.grade]`, so the pair is the STRONGEST-GRADED mention, a tie between equal grades goes to whichever row the scan returned first (the query orders by `capture_sha` only, so within a capture the order is unspecified), and `schema.mjs`'s own `connections` comment says so in words. No op, column or state lets anyone choose, and nothing on the row says the pair was not chosen. (3) **THE CONSEQUENCE IS IN THE OVERCLAIMING DIRECTION, DRIVEN 2026-09-18 (M-51, `node tools/fw21-onpoint-probe.mjs`) through `op=promote`/`op=resolve`/`op=connect`/`op=connections&content=`:** document A resolves to one ordinance TWICE at grade A (a reference read on p.2 and another read on p.9), document B once (p.5). The pair keeps p.2. A member's citation of PAGE 9 of A — where the ordinance genuinely IS, at grade A — answers `connection_grade: null`, `outside: 1` (`CONNECTION_PAIR_OUTSIDE_EXTENT`, C-49.1) and the sentence *"all 1 of this document's connections were established by references read outside page 9, so none of them reaches this citation"*. **A citation of page 7, which never mentions the ordinance, answers with byte-identical counts** — the record cannot tell the two apart. Flipping the collapse's `>` to `>=` (a tie-break carrying no relevance input) moves the pair to p.9 and the `outside` to p.2. **That is a definite NO the record cannot support**: the truth is that the record chose a different mention by grade and never asked which one is on point, which is UNDETERMINED. `reading_refs` is keyed `(capture_sha, ref)`, so a SINGLE reference string repeated on several pages is held at ONE position (`INSERT OR REPLACE`), so the same shape looks reachable with a single ref — READ from the schema and the writer, NOT driven. **What closing it takes, and it is not one owner's:** RECORD — `connectionGradeForContent` must not say `outside` while another resolution of the same capture to the same entity is placed inside the extent (that alone is small, a C-49 arm plus a suite ground, and it is the overclaim fix); the pair must say HOW it was chosen (`strongest-graded` today) so a reader never takes a grade collapse for a relevance judgement; and the member's act that CHOOSES the on-point pair (an I5/I3 change, its own IC) with a UI affordance on the connection. **REC-86 does NOT cover it**: REC-86 is NARROW of a basis LEG, and a connection is a derived edge between two captures that no leg names. Part II §14.5, §17 and §18 said "rowed (REC-86)" and were corrected by FW-21 in the same commit. **NARROWED AGAIN 2026-09-18 by REC-120 (IC-129), acts (1) and (2) of the three above — driven through `op=promote`/`op=resolve`/`op=connect`/`op=connections`, suite `bio-plane/test/rec120-onpoint-undetermined.test.mjs`.** (1) `connectionGradeForContent` reads EVERY resolution of the cited capture to the connection's entity (with its `reading_refs` position) and answers UNDETERMINED under a new C-49.4 `CONNECTION_PAIR_MENTION_UNCHOSEN`, naming the mentions, where a definite answer would rest on the pair alone: a pair OUTSIDE the part while another mention is inside or cannot be placed (M-51's page 9 now reads `undetermined: 1`, page 7 still `outside: 1` under C-49.1 with its pre-item sentence), and — required by REC-120's own negative control, which the row's act (1) wording did not cover — a pair INSIDE the part that was kept only on a TIE over an equal-grade mention not inside it (M-51's page 2, which FW-21's probe used as its ground, now reads UNDETERMINED: its "reach" flipped with a one-character tie-break and so carried no relevance). A weaker mention outside does not unsettle a reach; a one-mention document is byte-identical (golden taken on the pristine tree). (2) The pair says what it is: `connections.pair_rule` (I5) records `strongest-graded/first-reference-by-sort`, the scan now orders `capture_sha, ref` so the tie-break EXISTS rather than being SQLite's row order, the row's basis says "strongest-graded mention … not one chosen as on point", and `determining_pair.selection` (I3) publishes `{method, tie_break, chosen: false, says}` — `tie_break: null` with the reason stated on a row derived before REC-120. **What remains open is act (3) ONLY: the member's act of CHOOSING the on-point pair** (I5 + I3, RECORD + UI, its own IC), without which every multi-mention document's partial citations stay UNDETERMINED — honest, and the cost is that a member gets no definite answer where several mentions exist. | D-154 | design | 2026-08-01 | **A HUNCH is temporary declared bias with a temporarily-high connection grade, and it is bias debt that must clear before publication.** RULED 2026-08-01 (DEC-15), and it resolves the D1(a)-versus-D1(b) fork by showing it was a false one: an authored connection grade and an earned one are not rivals, they are two phases of the same object. During `open` a member may author a connection grade high enough to make otherwise disconnected evidence traversable — *"the investigator thinks this is true"* — and at `published` no hunch may remain, so every leg is earned or is honest testimony. **THE GATE ALREADY EXISTED AND WAS BUILT FOR SOMETHING ELSE:** `BIO_Declared_Bias_v0_1.md`'s bias-debt section already ends *"a work product carrying bias debt cannot advance its workproduct_state or be ratified for publication until the debt is settled"*, written for a bias statement CHANGING and leaving old analysis owed a re-run. Registering a hunch as what it is reaches that rule with no new machinery. It also settles what CLEARING means without a further ruling: debt is *cleared by re-running the evaluation under the current set*, so the test is neither "delete the leg" nor "leave it in unrated" — **the case must still hold when the hunch is removed from the set**. `grade_source` gains `hunch` beside `resolution` and `testimony`; it is the ONLY authored grade permitted above D, permitted precisely because it cannot survive publication; it carries an author and a date; and it is **visible as a hunch on every surface from the moment it is made**, because the failure mode this construct invites is a hunch quietly ageing into a fact. A hunch is NOT an `undetermined` leg and must never be composed as one — R1 suspends an axis when a grade is ABSENT, while a hunch grade is present and asserted, and treating it as undetermined would destroy the traversability the ruling exists to preserve. `op=publishpreflight` refuses `UNCLEARED_HUNCH` naming every leg, before any signature exists. Manifest registration needs `object_type: bias` (D-84) and is sequenced behind it; the leg-level source and the refusal ship with the claim layer where they bite. | M9 · CLOSED 2026-09-21 AS A DEBT ROW by LED-7 batch S10-2 (SCHEDULER #10) — the ruling is built and its remaining act is BACKLOG REC-15 (owner RECORD, M10, blocked on DEC-33's deferral), the pre-flight that reports `UNCLEARED_HUNCH`. Verified at `18d50de5`: `GRADE_SOURCES` carries `hunch` (`bio-plane/checks/bio-checks.mjs`), `node tools/status.mjs 7` reads *hunch debt: an uncleared hunch refuses publication* BUILT (`UNCLEARED_HUNCH`, `store.mjs`), and REC-11, REC-12, REC-18, UI-11 and UI-12 are done. **Prior disposition, kept verbatim:** M9 - open · REC-11, REC-12, REC-15, REC-18, UI-11, UI-12 |

| D-85 | design | 2026-07-30 | **An assistant-surfaced focus must carry the bias manifest in force when it was surfaced, and nothing does.** Follows from two facts that met this session. Bob ruled an assistant may open a focus unattended; the bias doctrine says any BIO work done under bias carries the fully declared bias as part of that work's evidentiary record. The assistant is doing BIO work, the effective statement set shaped what it scrutinised and which inferences it could draw, and unlike a member it will not remember. Without the manifest a focus surfaced under one lens is indistinguishable later from one surfaced under another, and bias debt cannot be computed against it. This also answers a question the doctrine leaves open: an assistant has no bias of its own to declare, it has the effective set it was run under plus the objective it was pursuing, and recording both is the whole of its obligation. | M4 · CLOSED 2026-09-21 AS A DEBT ROW by SCHEDULER #10, draining BOB #25's inbox entry (items 2 and 3) — PLACED as a BACKLOG task under its own id, D-85 (owner RECORD, M4), directly after REC-165, narrowed by BOB #25 to `INVESTIGATIVE-SESSION.md` §11 item 5 rules 2 and 3. | D-75 | design | 2026-07-30 | **The content framework and the object catalogue have been developed independently and have never been connected.** Framework §12, added at Bob's direction that the system must support goals, objectives and aspirations: the record already models intent as `project` (which carries an `objective` field, states `forming → investigating → matured → closed`), `focus` and `problem` (open questions and obstacles), and `action` (steps). The framework was written through eleven sections without touching any of it. Two object types genuinely do not exist and are needed: ASPIRATION, a standing commitment that never closes and sets priority, and GOAL, a bounded pursuit that eventually does. Conflating the three is the ordinary failure: an aspiration written as an objective is never finished and demoralises, and an objective written as an aspiration is never checked and quietly abandons itself. | M4 · CLOSED 2026-09-21 BY THE THIRD DOOR, SCHEDULER #10 draining BOB #25's answer to Q1 of DELEGATION 2026-09-21 SCHEDULER (#10) -> BOB (`37367611`): a STATED DEFERRAL in its home design, read there: `BIO_Content_Framework_v0_10.md`'s front matter (Incomplete sections), §12, §12.1, §12.2, *THE INTENT LAYER'S OBJECTS ARE A STATED DEFERRAL, with the trigger that schedules them*; ASPIRATION and GOAL as objects are designed at concept level and absent at the code (`node tools/status.mjs 8`), and its trigger: SCHEDULER schedules the design act when `node tools/status.mjs 12` reads the publication ceremony and the accept surface BUILT, both ABSENT today, or earlier on Bob's word. **Prior disposition, kept verbatim:** M4 · open — **SENT 2026-09-21 by SCHEDULER #10 to BOB, Q1 of a group of three (DELEGATION 2026-09-21 SCHEDULER (#10) -> BOB)**, verified at the code on `7a8b81d6`. |
| D-76 | design | 2026-07-30 | **Progress on an objective is reported rather than derived, and it should be computed from the record.** Framework §12's load-bearing idea: an objective states its SATISFACTION CONDITION in the framework's own vocabulary, e.g. "hold a Grade B or better procurement progression instance for every contract over $250k on fund 3100 since FY2019".
