| D-192 | defect | 2026-08-04 | **Integrity of stored bytes is not integrity of what a reader SEES, and archived-page replay is a documented attack surface.** Lerner, Kohno & Roesner, *Rewriting History: Changing the Archived Web from the Present* (ACM CCS 2017, adalerner.com/lerner-rewritingHistory-CCS17.pdf): four demonstrated attacks that alter a reader's view of an archived page — text, images, styling and behaviour — by injecting or abusing script that runs at VIEW time, and the paper's own summary is that *"attackers do not need to compromise the archives in order to compromise users' views of a stored page."* BIO stores captured HTML and renders it, and the whole product is trustworthiness of the record, so a reader shown an altered rendition of bytes we hold intact is the defect class this project cares about most — the hash stays correct while the claim the reader forms is wrong. NOT YET ASSESSED against our own viewer: what BIO renders, with what sandboxing, and whether any captured script executes at view time is UNMEASURED and is the first step. The mitigation vocabulary is standard (sandboxed iframes, CSP, stripping or neutering script at RENDER time while never altering the STORED bytes — the stored/served distinction is the load-bearing one, since altering stored bytes would break the hash and the premise). PRIOR DISPOSITION (verbatim): M2 - open · measure what the viewer executes before designing the fix | CLOSED 2026-09-23 AS A DEBT ROW — PLACED by LED-7 batch S17-1 (SCHEDULER #17) as the plan row D-192 in `BACKLOG.md` (same id), its fix, suite and negative control named there. |
| D-204 | defect | 2026-08-05 | **No op can move an Information document off `verified` except to `retired`, which is terminal and means something else.** MEASURED 2026-08-05 by REC-54 while dispositioning D-200. `op=reopen` takes an INQUIRY target only; `op=retire` moves `verified -> retired` and says in its own refusal *"There is no move back out of retired"*; `op=release` moves `collected -> verified` and is explicitly not repeatable. So when D-200 says a bundle whose chain cannot be reconstructed should be *"moved BACK OFF verified"*, the plane offers no act that does it, and the only available exit permanently withdraws the document — which asserts something quite different from *"we cannot show this route"*. REC-54 did not need the route (all ten of D-200's bundles reconstructed from recorded evidence) but the gap is real and the NEXT such bundle will need it. **This is a DOCTRINE question and is routed as one, not decided by a worker:** retracting a verification is a statement about a group's own past act, and whether the record may un-say it — versus carrying a standing `undetermined` marker at `verified` — is Bob's call. NEEDS: a decision between a `verified -> collected` retraction edge with a required reason, and an `undetermined`-route marker that leaves the state alone. PRIOR DISPOSITION (verbatim): M2 · open — DOCTRINE (Bob), raised by REC-54 as a decision item | CLOSED 2026-09-23 IN FACT by LED-7 batch S17-1 (SCHEDULER #17), door 1 — Bob ruled the principle under DEC-56 (2026-08-06) and the option built is the standing marker that leaves the document's state alone: REC-63's `provenanceroute` write (schema THE STANDING MARKER) and REC-116's `provenanceroutes` read (IC-120), refusals catalogued in bio-checks; no verified-to-collected edge is owed under that ruling. Read at the code on `02603e88`. |
| D-247 | defect | 2026-08-08 | **THE NEGATIVE-CONTROL REGISTER DOES NOT READ FLEET SUITES AT ALL, AND `--strict` PRINTS 100% WHILE SAYING SO IN A NUMBER NOBODY READS AS A GAP.** Found 2026-08-08 by CPDF-12, and found by MEASURING the instrument rather than by reading it: this item added `pdf-worker/test/pagepixels.test.mjs` with a SEVEN-ARM `NEGATIVE CONTROL:` declaration, and `REGISTER_FLOOR` did not move — `arms 557/557 · classified 128/128 · corpus 129/129` before and after, byte for byte. **CONTROL RUN: the whole declaration block was stripped from the new suite and `node scripts/coverage.mjs --strict` STILL EXITED 0**, with the register still printing `129 of 129 suites declare one (100.0%)`. (Restore verified by sha256 — 20,492 B, `9868c684…` — and by `git status` reading clean.) **The corpus line already says it in plain sight and is easy to read as reassurance rather than as scope: `129 plane suite(s) · 4 fleet suite(s)`. The register's corpus is the 129.** What DOES exist is a per-MEMBER check in the FLEET section (`2/2 declaring a negative control`), and that is a weaker claim in the exact generous direction this project treats as the worse one: a member passes it if ANY ONE of its suites declares a control, so a member with four suites and one declaration reads `control declared`. **This is D-117's fleet blind spot still open on the CONTROLS axis after M0-5 closed it on the OPS axis** — coverage names three surfaces and the fleet is now held to one and a half of them. | D-292 | defect | 2026-08-10 | **EVERY SERVICE BINDING IN THE REPOSITORY NAMES A WORKER THAT DOES NOT EXIST, AND CLOUDFLARE REFUSES THE DEPLOY THAT NEEDS IT.** MEASURED by deploying the fleet: `pdf-worker` went up clean (it declares no service binding) and **`agent-worker` was REFUSED outright** — *"Service binding 'PLANE' references Worker 'bio-plane' which was not found"* (code 10143). Measured, that alone would make every plane deploy FAIL the same way, because the declared target does not resolve. The fix is derivation WITH THE INSTANCE SLUG SUBSTITUTED — a binding must point at the instance being installed, not at a name in a file. **That is DS-1's real content** (the installer wires the fleet to THIS instance) and it is why DS-2's version-authority row and DS-4's gated deploy could not have worked first. So the fleet's uninstallability was written down twice, by the item that built it, and enacted zero times. **That is the queue's own "a note is not an item" class** — the lesson recorded on 2026-08-05 and again on 2026-08-10 — and the Cloudflare refusal above is its first MECHANICAL proof: until something tried to deploy, two accurate notes and a live delegation produced exactly the same result as knowing nothing. **The corroborating evidence was also already live and nobody had read it as evidence:** the `civicos` worker's one binding is `service PLANE -> biosmoke7` — the only worker in the account wired to the plane points at the INSTANCE, not at `bio-plane`, which is the rule this row prescribes, already working in production since 2026-08-05. PRIOR DISPOSITION (verbatim): M1 · **DEPLOY HALF CLOSED 2026-09-10, INSTALLER HALF IS D-297** — `tools/deploy-fleet.mjs` templates every service target from the instance slug and `agent-worker` IS NOW DEPLOYED: the account read back `service PLANE -> biosmoke7`, and it serves `{"ok":true,"name":"agent-worker","version":"0.1.0"}`. **THE FLEET IS FULLY DEPLOYED FOR THE FIRST TIME** — agent-worker, biosmoke7, civicos, newgroup, pdf-worker. The tracked configs still say `bio-plane` and that is CORRECT: the phantom is substituted at deploy, never written down. | D-300 | defect | 2026-09-10 | **A TEST ESTATE THAT READS THE WALL CLOCK CAN GO RED WITHOUT ANYONE TOUCHING IT, AND THE CENSUS THAT SAYS IT WON'T IS A FLOOR RATHER THAN A CLEARANCE.** Opened by M0-22, which fixed the one LIVE instance: `action-loop.test.mjs` pinned `DUE = "2026-09-10"`, the calendar reached it, and C-11.1 began reporting every action fixture in the file silently past-due — **73 pass / 6 FAIL on a tree nobody had touched**. The shape of that failure is the reason for this row: **CONDUCT measured `168/168 · 10,351` that morning and a later worker measured `167/168 · 10,345` on the SAME COMMIT, and both were honest.** A figure falsified by TIME is indistinguishable from one falsified by a CHANGE, so re-measurement — this project's standing defence against stale numbers — cannot detect it, and points the wrong way exactly as the missing-`node_modules` trap does. **WHAT WAS MEASURED, DRIVEN RATHER THAN GREPPED.** `bio-plane/test/clockshift.preload.mjs` advances node's `Date.now()`/`new Date()` and is inherited by spawned children (proved by probe, not assumed: child 2027, parent 2026). **215 suites were run under an advanced clock — 168 battery suites at +1, +5 and +20 years, plus the 47 `civicos-ui` harness suites at +1 year, which the battery never runs.** Result: **ZERO further live instances at any horizon.** Four suites (`governor`, `scheduler`, `selection`, `task-drain-alarm`) fail under any node-side shift and were **cleared by measurement, not by judgement — they fail identically at +1 HOUR**, a shift that cannot overtake a calendar date; they compare a node-computed now to a workerd-written stamp, so they are sensitive to the instrument's own node/workerd skew. **WHY THE ZERO IS A FLOOR AND MUST NEVER BE QUOTED AS A CLEARANCE, stated as the instrument's limits:** (1) **it does not reach workerd at all** — miniflare runs the plane in a child process whose clock is the true wall, so `store.mjs`'s `#nowMs()` fallback is invisible to it, and **only 3 of 260 `bio-plane/test` files bind `BIO_NOW_MS`** (`overdue-successor`, `capture-progressions`, and now `action-loop`) while **only 5 inject `nowMs` into `checkBundle`** — the catalog holds five `ctx.nowMs ?? Date.now()` sites (reeval age, two monitor-staleness, package age, C-11.1 past-due) and 255 files consult them through the wall; (2) a fixture dated beyond +20 years is not reached; (3) `newgroup/test` is DIST's and was not run; (4) **the instrument sees FAILURES, not FINDINGS** — an assertion shaped as `.some(...)` over a message list absorbs a new finding without failing, so a suite can acquire the defect and stay green under this sweep. **WHAT CLOSING IT TAKES:** bind `BIO_NOW_MS` in every suite that constructs a Miniflare and thread `nowMs` into every `checkBundle` call in a suite — mechanical, ~160 sites, and it makes the class unrepresentable estate-wide the way M0-22 made it unrepresentable in one file. The cheaper partial: a battery arm that runs the clock-advanced sweep on a cadence so a latent fixture is found by the instrument rather than by the calendar. **Do NOT close it by moving dates further out** — M0-22 measured that fix directly and it is green today and RED at +1 year, reproducing the original signature exactly, with a viable window of NINE DAYS before it needs doing again. Relation: C-11.1, REC-8's injectable clock, REC-24. | D-357 | defect | 2026-09-14 | **THE DEC-49 GUARD'S REGION MATCHER ENDS IN `\\b`, AND A HYPHEN SATISFIES A WORD BOUNDARY, SO ONE REGION'S OPENER COUNTS AS ANOTHER'S WHENEVER THE FIRST NAME IS A HYPHEN-PREFIX OF THE SECOND.** Found by CONDUCT #11 at FW-17's integration (2026-09-14): `civicos-ui/check-refusal-codes.mjs`'s `REGION_START(name)` is `/DEC-49 REGION\\s+(name)\\b/` — for REC-84's region `is-content-row` it matched FW-17's new region `is-content-row-present` (`\\b` holds between `w` and `-`), the guard reported *found 2 opening marker(s), expected exactly 1* and FAILED THE HARNESS on a correct tree; neither worker could have seen it (each branch had one of the two names), so it is the class of defect only the integrator meets. RESOLVED AT THE INSTANCE by renaming FW-17's region to `pair-content-row-present` at its three sites (two markers, one `where`). WHAT IT COSTS WHILE OPEN: any future region whose name extends an existing one by a hyphenated suffix fails the harness at merge time with a message that says DUPLICATE, and the rename is the only cure the message suggests. WHAT CLOSING IT TAKES: anchor the matcher to the WHOLE name (a lookahead for whitespace, `*/` or end of comment after the name, not `\\b`), with a negative control that plants a hyphen-extended sibling region and proves the guard counts ONE opener per name; `regionLines` floors unmoved. | D-218 | design | 2026-08-05 | **UNMEASURED: whether a fleet-member Worker can hold a WHOLE investigative run inside the paid CPU ceiling.** Raised by DEC-60's integration architecture (`INVESTIGATIVE-SESSION.md` §14a), where Bob's Claude-account cascade (member, else project, else instance) settles that the agent loop runs in the GROUP'S OWN infrastructure — which preserves the installer's sovereignty promise and points at the fleet-member shape `pdf-worker` already established (I6). **THE REASONING THAT MUST NOT BE TRUSTED UNTIL MEASURED:** an agent loop spends most of its time WAITING on API responses, and Workers bill CPU rather than wall time, so a run of many minutes may sit well inside the ceiling. Nothing left to measure. Read at the code on `02603e88`. |
| D-222 | design | 2026-08-06 | **THE MEANING LAYER HAS NO QUERY SURFACE, and the investigative session is the first consumer that genuinely needs one — so this is a PRECONDITION of DEC-60, not an enhancement.** Raised 2026-08-06 by the rigorous architecture pass Bob asked for. **THE MEASUREMENT, from `STORE-AS-CACHE.md` and re-verified:** BIO has two retrieval routes. **Route 1**, the query compiler (`query.mjs`), projects **34 filterable fields and exactly 5 FTS columns** (`title`, `body`, `meta`, `locator`, `authority`). **Route 2**, the meaning tables — `readings`, `reading_refs`, `resolutions`, `connections`, `inquiry_basis` — is **not reachable by the query compiler at all.** **WHY THE HOLE IS INVISIBLE, which is worse than the hole:** route 1 carries SCALAR SUMMARIES of the meaning layer onto the bundle row (`capture` → `inquiry_capture_strength`, `legs` → `inquiry_basis_count`, `connection` → `inquiry_connection_strength`, `resolution` → `action_resolution`), so a caller can filter by a finding's STRENGTH and never reach the legs that produced it. `STORE-AS-CACHE.md`'s own words: *the projection creates a false sense of coverage — the meaning layer is visible as a number and unreachable as a structure.* **WHY IT BLOCKS THE SESSION:** forming a version of a claim's legs (DEC-60) is meaning-layer work end to end. As `INVESTIGATIVE-SESSION.md` §3 is written, the session's read scope is NOT ACHIEVABLE — it can see that a claim scores 0.7 and cannot see what it rests on, which is precisely the work it exists to do. **RELATED AND SAME-SHAPED: D-220**, where sixty versions of one document read as sixty documents is the identical false-coverage failure one axis over. **RESEARCHED IN DEPTH 2026-08-06 (sub-session), and the gap is wider than this row stated.** **(i) THIRTEEN TABLES, and about TWENTY-FOUR ops read them — NONE of which is a query surface.** Every one is a fixed-shape lookup keyed on exactly one of `capture_sha`, `ref`, `entity_id`, `relation_id`, `bundle_id`, `target_id` or `(progression_key, entity_id)`, or an unkeyed whole-table walk with a fixed output shape. **No predicate, no projection choice, no sort, no paging** (the one exception is `documentsNamingEntity`'s clamped limit). So the layer is not under-queried; it is UNQUERIED BY CONSTRUCTION. **(ii) GRADE IS A FILTER NOWHERE.** No op filters on grade at all, and **no grade column is indexed** — there is no `resolutions(grade)` or `connections(grade)`. The consequence with teeth: the C tier is explicitly *"FLAGGED for a member to confirm"* (`schema.mjs:876`) and **nothing can enumerate the flagged set** — `connectionsFor` refuses without a key (`store.mjs:8618`). A queue of work the system creates and cannot list. **(iii) NO CROSS-AXIS JOIN.** *"Every claim whose legs cite documents from authority X"* is two indexed columns and no join. **(iv) TIME IS A PREDICATE NOWHERE** — `at` is on nearly every table and filterable on none, so *"what resolved this week"* is unaskable. **(v) `readings.reading` IS AN OPAQUE JSON BLOB**, so nothing inside a reading is reachable except what `reading_refs`/`reading_ref_terms` already project out. **(vi) SEE D-223** — the sharpest single instance: hunch debt is publication-disqualifying and cannot be listed. NEEDS: a meaning-layer read surface. **Whether it extends the query compiler or is a second addressable surface is NOT a free choice — `query.mjs:701-705` states that a second query path is what the compiler's design exists to prevent, and D-15 gives visibility exactly ONE compilation point, enforced by a throw rather than a convention.** Options are being graded; D-223's question is acceptance on whichever wins. **Stage C DESIGNED 2026-09-14 (BOB #11): `docs/development/CONTENT-SEARCH-DESIGN.md` §4.2 — the `content:` and `passage:` arms and their row shapes on the one compiler; D-225's caps are its item 1, the arms items 2 and 5.** PRIOR DISPOSITION (verbatim): M3 · open (DEC-60) — **BLOCKS the investigative session's read scope**; nothing else in the IS decomposition depends on it. Appended 2026-08-07: the options were GRADED 2026-08-06 in `INVESTIGATIVE-SESSION.md` §14c — recommendation D, staged as A then C; do not re-grade — **ROUTED 2026-09-14 by CONDUCT #11 at the BOB #11 drain: stage C (content-grain search) is DESIGNED in `CONTENT-SEARCH-DESIGN.md` and rowed as REC-90 (the `content:` arm) → REC-91 (the text index) → REC-92 (the `passage:` arm), behind M0-31's measurement; D-225's caps are REC-89.** | CLOSED 2026-09-23 IN FACT by LED-7 batch S17-2 (SCHEDULER #17), door 1 — both stages of §14c are built: stage A (PL-8, PL-9; `status.mjs` 9.meaning-search BUILT) and stage C (REC-89 to REC-92, done; 9.content-search BUILT). `INVESTIGATIVE-SESSION.md`'s front matter still calls stage C not queued; its correction is sent to BOB. For `CCITTFaxDecode` the page's own `/Rotate` is applied exactly — a quarter turn lands a bit on a bit — and the result is `upright: true`. For `DCTDecode` the route hands on the publisher's own JPEG bytes UNTOUCHED, which is both the cheapest path and the strongest provenance position available (no transform of ours sits between the record and the pixels a reader checks a claim against); rotating it means decoding and re-encoding, which throws that away and is lossy besides. **MEASURED SIZE OF THE GAP: 3 of the 24 image-only pages are `DCTDecode` at `/Rotate 270`** (14 are `DCTDecode` at `/Rotate 0` and need nothing; 7 are `CCITTFaxDecode` at `/Rotate 270` and are handled). Those three come back `upright: false` with `rotate_deg` STATED — never silently sideways. **WHY IT MATTERS RATHER THAN BEING TIDINESS: a sideways page fed to an OCR engine does not fail, it INVENTS.** The same exhibit at `/Rotate 270`, un-rotated, scored **8.67% character accuracy with 355 MINTED DIGITS** in fluent confident prose and the engine announced nothing (MEASUREMENTS 2026-08-08); upright it scored 99.93% / 90-of-90 / 1. NEEDS one of: (a) a baseline JPEG decoder in the member (~600 lines, and it makes the route lossy — the honest cost is that the record then holds OUR re-encoding, so `text_source` would have to say so); (b) a LOSSLESS JPEG transform (a quarter turn is expressible as an MCU-block permutation for dimensions that are multiples of the MCU size, which these are not guaranteed to be); (c) the CONSUMER rotates, which is the cheapest and is what `upright: false` is for — CPDF-10 is the first consumer and this is a real input to its design. Relation: CPDF-12, CPDF-10, DEC-42. PRIOR DISPOSITION (verbatim): M2 · open — **decide it in CPDF-10, where the consumer is.** Nothing is silently wrong today: the field says so. | CLOSED 2026-09-23 IN FACT by LED-7 batch S17-2 (SCHEDULER #17), door 1 — CPDF-10 decided it: `ocr-worker/src/transcribe.mjs` refuses every `passthrough-dct` page (PIXELS_UNREADABLE), so no sideways JPEG reaches OCR. What remains is the missing JPEG decoder, D-320, whose row now carries the rotation note. Read at the code on `02603e88`. |

| D-241 | gap | 2026-08-08 | **`op=connections` CANNOT TELL A CALLER THAT THE DERIVATION BEHIND ITS ROWS WAS BOUNDED — only that its own READ was.** Raised by REC-66 in the same turn as the fix that creates the condition, rather than left implicit at the site. `op=connect` now bounds the DERIVATION (D-224's k(k-1)/2 growth, measured at 398 MB and 10.3 s for one subject at k=1,000) and publishes `limit`/`document_limit`/`truncated` on ITS OWN ANSWER, so the caller who asked for the derivation is told exactly what was and was not worked out. **A LATER READER IS NOT.** `op=connections` answers `truncated: false` over a complete read of an INCOMPLETE graph, and the two are indistinguishable to it: every row it returns is true, and the set is the pairs among the first N documents by capture_sha rather than among all of them. This is under-claiming rather than over-claiming — the safe direction, and CLAUDE.md's rule is nonetheless that *undetermined is first-class and must be STATED*. NEEDS: either the derivation's extent recorded where the read can see it (a per-entity stamp of the document bound the last derivation applied, which is one additive column and a write already in the path), or `op=connections` computing the same fact on read (a second scan, which is the cost this item exists to avoid). The first is cheap and honest; it was NOT taken here because it touches `schema.mjs`, which this item did not claim and which two other workers are in. Relation: D-224 (the growth), D-227 (the instrument half), REC-66's IC-35. | D-242 | gap | 2026-08-08 | **THE MINTED-ID LEDGER (M0-17) IS SCOPED TO ONE CLONE ON ONE MACHINE, AND NOTHING SAYS SO AT THE MOMENT IT STOPS BEING TRUE.** `tools/mintid.mjs` takes an id by an exclusive create under `git rev-parse --git-common-dir` — the ONE `.git` every worktree of this checkout shares. That is deliberately the exact scope the collisions have: **every colliding pair on 2026-08-08 was two worktrees of one clone**, which is why this is the right mechanism today rather than a network allocator nobody would run. **What it does NOT cover: two clones, a CI runner, a second machine, or a network filesystem whose `O_EXCL` is not atomic (NFSv2 was not; APFS and ext4 are).** In any of those the ledgers are independent and two allocators can take the same number — back to the read-the-file convention, which is the graceful floor and not a silent failure, because the corpus floor still holds. **THE REAL COST IS NOT THE COLLISION, IT IS THE CONFIDENCE**: a worker that ran the tool believes its id is safe, and in a two-clone world it would be wrong for a reason no output mentions. NEEDS: either the tool DETECTS that its ledger is not the only one (there is no cheap local test for that, which is why it is a debt and not a fix), or the day workers run on more than one clone the allocator moves to something with a single writer — the plane's own `Store.allocId` already is one, under `ctx.storage.transactionSync`, and is the obvious destination. Relation: M0-17, D-238 (the stash defect, the same shared-common-gitdir fact from the painful side). **NARROWED 2026-08-08 (D-243's item), and the row's own sentence is what got tested: "there is no cheap local test for that" is TRUE of the two-clone half and was NOT true of the non-atomic-filesystem half.** `mint` now PROBES the real ledger directory before it trusts it — an exclusive create, a second exclusive create that must be refused `EEXIST`, and a re-read proving the first writer's bytes did not move — and **REFUSES `EXCL_NOT_HONOURED` rather than minting** if any of the three fails. That closes the NFSv2 line by measurement instead of accepting it on report. **The two-clone line is NOT closed and cannot be locally: nothing on this machine can see a second ledger.** What changed for it is that **the confidence is no longer silent** — every mint, every `--list` and every `--audit` now prints a SCOPE block naming what the take IS exclusive against (every worktree of this clone) and what it is NOT (any allocator on a different ledger), with a WARN when the ledger has been used from more than one host (shared storage, where a one-process probe cannot speak for cross-host atomicity) or when `BIO_IDALLOC_DIR` overrides the git-derived scope. **The decision, recorded: REFUSE when exclusivity is demonstrably absent, WARN and still mint when the scope is wider than what was tested, STATE always — because the happy path is where a false belief is formed.** **Still open:** two clones cannot be detected, and `--audit` section 2 reports an allocation above the ledger's high-water as a QUESTION precisely because a second ledger is one of its two explanations. PRIOR DISPOSITION (verbatim): M0 · open — **WATCH, no task, and NARROWED rather than closed.** The trigger is unchanged and concrete: the first time a worker runs anywhere but a worktree of `/Users/sparky/ClaudeCodeBIO/bio`. | CLOSED 2026-09-23 AS A DEBT ROW — PLACED by LED-7 batch S17-2 (SCHEDULER #17) as the plan row D-242 in `BACKLOG.md` (same id), its fix, suite and negative control named there. |
| D-64 | gap | 2026-07-30 | **A client-rendered page is recognised as a shell and still cannot be captured as evidence.** `docprofile`'s `client_rendered` handler detects the case structurally (a body with no links and no prose) and carries a member-facing warning that what was collected is the frame and not the figures. That is the honest interim and not the requirement. The standing ruling is that JS-rendered content IS the content and must be captured at the SAME GRADE as the rest of the document, because the render happened in the site's own execution environment at capture time while the HTML/CSS rendition is rendered later in the reader's. That needs the Browser Rendering path, which is available on Workers Free at 10 minutes a day. Until it lands, `oaklandca.opengov.com` and every portal like it can be captured and cannot be cited, and the record should say so on the document page rather than only at capture time.
